# Omni ledger: Harbor research

The single work ledger for the research program. Edit `omni-ledger.json`; this document is generated.

A source-reported proof, run, or completion is not a fresh verification. Current records preserve assumptions, open acceptance conditions, and evidence separately. The roadmap registry remains the repository scheduling authority; this ledger does not start jobs or authorize spend.

## Authority and decisions

- Operator directive, 2026-09-30: consolidate the research record; retire supporting documents after extraction; retain the eight papers and their publication PDFs.
- omni-ledger.json is the research work authority. OMNI-LEDGER.md and program.json are projections. Source transcripts and the immutable ZIP are evidence, not active instructions or a second backlog.
- The repository roadmap registry still owns scheduling and gate registration. The local Port Daddy runtime remains halted; the hosted Work Register rejected unauthenticated read access. No live claim or scheduling authority is inferred.
- An imported DONE, proved, verified or measured label retains its source context. Revalidation, merge, deployment and empirical utility are separate evidence states.
- The current-work records NEXT-P1 through NEXT-P8 set the research development direction. Archived critique and section records preserve all objections and rationale without automatically reopening them.
- Exact original Markdown and PDF bytes, including graphics, remain recoverable in omni-sources.zip. Searchable transcripts do not replace the original mathematical layout.
- Active paper TeX, publication PDFs, code, data, formal models, figures, and executable skill/study contracts remain live. Build outputs are restricted to the eight papers so supporting PDFs do not reappear.

## Record details

<a id="work-book-research-uptake"></a>
### BOOK-RESEARCH-UPTAKE: Book uptake of the research: coverage, corrections and useful imports

**Status:** twelve scoped source corrections and local publication reconciliation complete; hosted checks pending. **Kind:** source and publication assessment.

**Result or question:** Current reconciliation: the seven named mathematical/scope conflicts have source patches and bounded checks. Chapters 5, 6 and 8 now develop safe continuation, finite reachable-family admission and least-cost categorical certificates. The rebuilt 592-page Book has been inspected at the changed claims; the source/PDF hashes and exact text searches are recorded in PROGRAM-RECONCILIATION. Hosted checks remain pending. Remaining research imports retain their open status. The assessment below is the hash-bound audit snapshot; its findings are superseded only by explicit resolutions.

The research already supplies much of the Book's formal backbone. The largest remaining value is to make evidence conditions operational, repair inconsistent statements, and carry source claims into the actual publication artifact.

#### Scope and defensible coverage

The authoritative corpus is the eight chapters selected by `whitepaper/textbook.json`. The Swiss, maritime and technical roots use the same generated chapter body, so they are three presentations of one argument. This assessment covers those sources and the checked-in 589-page public Book PDF; it does not certify an external worktree or a deployed website.

All 17 R1–R17 index entries name a chapter. Of 34 total indexed entries, 20 have chapter locators and 14 Paper 8 entries have none. These are structural counts, not percentages of scientific correctness or pedagogical adoption. In particular, R17 and CR are marked `standalone-only` while also having chapter locations; the inspected chapter text takes precedence over that status field. The core is frequently developed through statements, proofs, examples and exercises, rather than merely cited. Paper 8's typed two-complex, relative extension and report-error distance remain substantial opportunities.

#### Chapter-by-chapter assessment

| Chapter | What is actually incorporated | What importing more research would let the reader do |
|---|---|---|
| 1. The Single-Writer Kernel | Strong: controllability, partial observation, work-unit invariants and mutation witnesses (R5, R8). | Turn incomplete observations into an adapter-specific safe-action rule; connect the unknown-effect warning to a worked retry/refusal case. |
| 2. The Anchor Protocol | Applies the control boundary to capability admission; its own attenuation proof estate also lies outside R1–R17. | Bind admission to the exact evidence snapshot and current authority epoch; show why an authentic claim can still be stale or insufficient. |
| 3. The Sealed Harbor | Strong: finite noninterference, atomic privacy-budget accounting, canary power and sequential detection (R9–R11). | Import the explicit payload-release factorization test; a small output alphabet alone does not establish release of the permitted information. |
| 4. The Legible Swarm | Extensive: digest floors, split preferences, decision loss, zoom, escalation, specialization and paging (R1–R4, R14–R16); several statements need correction. | Correct formula/hypothesis mismatches first; then teach witness-preserving summaries and complementary evidence acquisition under a human inspection budget. |
| 5. From Spawn to Person | Strong: no-mint transfer, engine substitution, migration, probation and inspection incentives (R7, R12, R13, B6). | Separate liability continuity from safe execution continuity; give an adapter table for atomic dedup/result, fenced readback, and unresolved remote effects. |
| 6. The Harbor Economy | Strong: specialization/succession and a tractable deontic fragment (R15, R17). | Add future-fact admission conditions and distinguish deciding existence of conflict from enumerating every witness; the latter must pay for output size. |
| 7. The Bonded Commons | Substantial institutional and settlement proof content; inspection-game calibration and evidence selection are only partially connected. | Derive an audit rate from the stated gain, detection and penalty model; choose cheap checkable dispute certificates without calling known-answer selection an optimal query policy. |
| 8. The Federated Harbor | Current source substantially incorporates Paper 7: visibility, sender blind spots, residual scope, robustness and marginal receipt value. Typed Paper 8 constructions and exact categorical certificates are not developed. | Add a typed worked example, exact categorical contradictions, partial-section extension and edge-group error distance; use them to design observations for a declared fault model. |

#### Corrections before expansion

1. **Decision odds, Chapter 4 / Paper 1.** The displayed expected-cost rule is `Cmiss*a >= catt + Cfa*(1-a)`. When `Cmiss > catt`, its odds denominator is `Cmiss-catt`, not `Cmiss`. At `Cmiss=10, catt=1, Cfa=1, a=0.17`, passing costs 1.70 and inspecting costs 1.83, but the printed odds rule orders inspection. Handle equality and degenerate costs explicitly rather than dividing through a nonpositive denominator. Source: `legible-swarm.tex` lines 2205–2226; `paper1.tex` lines 125–136.
2. **Strict zoom savings, Chapter 4 / Paper 1.** The displayed guaranteed-advantage expression equals one at density `d=1/12`. Strict improvement requires `d<1/12`, hence `f>12(p-delta)` for the stated composition; equality is break-even for that bound. Sources: Chapter 4 lines 1800–1888 and Paper 1 lines 214–228.
3. **Split-floor reasoning, Chapter 4 / Paper 1.** The shared-budget inequality survives, but the numerator-only binomial-growth explanation is false. Use the increasing summands of `F(t)=sum(i=0..t-1) log2((N-i)/(m-i))` for `2k<=m<N`. Do not infer that separate model processes or separate stored representations are necessary from a result about incompatible scalar orderings. Source: Chapter 4 lines 2447–2459; ledger `P1-SPLIT-CORRECTION`.
4. **Escalation hypotheses, Chapter 4.** Nondecreasing benefit and monotone validation do not imply a strictly increasing payoff or a unique exact root. For example, constant `b=V=1/2` and `delta*w=1` give zero payoff everywhere. Continuity, strictness, endpoint conditions and tie policy must match the claimed threshold theorem. Source: lines 1192–1211.
5. **Paging contract, Chapter 4.** The theorem uses `h` as pinned-set size while the detailed research bound uses `h` for offline comparator capacity and `p` for pins. A guarantee for the specified Landlord algorithm does not certify arbitrary recency-plus-pin policies. Align algorithm, comparator, pins and repair-on-touch assumptions before presenting the bound as one theorem. Source: lines 2514–2552 and `b9_context_paging.py` lines 17–32.
6. **Conflict enumeration, Chapter 6.** The every-witness interval bound omits emitted output. One scope with `n` obligations and `n` prohibitions yields `n^2` distinct clashes. Separate a yes/no or first-witness interface from full enumeration and include output cost. Source: `harbor-economy.tex` lines 786–800; ledger `P6-OUTPUT`.
7. **Continuity claim, Chapter 5.** Behavioral-equivalence language for replay outruns the nearby unmeasured-fidelity qualification. Score/obligation preservation does not establish correct state restoration or absence of duplicate external effects. Source: `spawn-to-person.tex` lines 844–905 and 1575–1638.

#### Publication artifact gap

The checked-in Book PDF, physical pages 459–461 (printed pages 441–443), asserts a `313/1101` pairwise-blind advantage and describes minimum adversary perturbation achievability too broadly. The current Chapter 8 source, lines 512–539 and 590–614, instead states zero residual-only and 209 direct-only cases under the equal-information synthetic comparison, and limits achievability to unrestricted edge-data correction. Those are different mathematical claims. Source incorporation therefore cannot be reported as PDF incorporation. Rebuild and inspect the Book after the scientific edits, then verify the same statements in each intended publication edition. No PDF was regenerated as part of this assessment.

#### Highest-value imports and their boundaries

- **Safe continuation (Chapters 1 and 5; `UNKNOWN-EFFECT`).** Give a decision table for retry, readback and refusal. A negative read does not justify retry while an earlier remote operation can still arrive. The finite one-operation/two-epoch model supports a bounded worked example; concurrent operations, retention windows and a real adapter require more work.
- **Future-safe commitment admission (Chapters 1 and 6; `FUTURE-HORN`).** Show a conflict that is dormant at admission and activated by a permitted fact. A greatest-future-fact closure is exact under fixed positive rules and a jointly reachable maximum. Mutually exclusive futures require reachable-state reasoning; their union can conservatively reject a policy that is safe in every reachable state.
- **Payload-aware release (Chapter 3; `RELEASE-FACTORIZATION`).** Teach the deterministic condition that the composed release must factor through the declared permitted observation. Then extend the model to submitted values, refusals, timing and adaptive traces before claiming a complete secrecy theorem.
- **Exact evidence certificates (Chapters 4, 7 and 8; `CLAIM-EXACT`, `CLAIM-COST`).** For registered categorical facts, test feasibility before a real-valued residual. A contradictory path can have zero linear residual. Two incompatible pins plus a zero-equality path give a checkable certificate; optimize its cost among known observations. Authentication, fixed fact/schema/snapshot and missingness are admission requirements.
- **Topology as an observation-design rule (Chapter 8; `P8-T3`, `P8-T4`, `P8-T6`).** Work through heterogeneous stalks and restriction maps, then show what declared redundancy buys: group distance greater than `k` detects every nonzero `k`-group error, and distance greater than `2k` identifies every such error modulo compatible data. In the graph model the latter is `lambda(G)>2k`. This concerns edge-report errors, not the identity or intention of an agent. Relative obstruction addresses extension of a visible compatible section; zero obstruction gives possible extension, not truth of unseen data. Adding a face without an independent observation does not add detection information.
- **Evidence acquisition (Chapters 4, 7 and 8; `P7-BUNDLES`, `NEXT-P7`).** Use two individually uninformative receipts whose combination resolves a question. This refutes zero-one-step-gain stopping. An optimal adaptive policy still needs answer distributions or an adversarial objective, prices, privacy constraints and stopping loss; the known-answer certificate optimizer does not supply it.
- **Calibrated institutional audit (Chapters 5 and 7; `R7`, `REVIEW-PILOT`).** Tie a rate and bond to an explicit gain, detection and slash convention. The chapter's 10% human-audit example is not itself a deterrence calculation. Correlated reviewers and wrongful penalties need measured, equal-budget cases; the prepared review protocol is not a completed experiment.

The main text should teach these as a small set of worked decisions, with exact hypotheses and witnesses beside each claim. Full algebra and exhaustive fixtures can sit in the research papers or appendices. Mathematical examples can be incorporated once their statements and independent checks agree; deployment and comparative-utility claims additionally require authenticated traces, adapter conformance and matched-information baselines.


**Assumptions:** Eight authoritative chapters and their shared visual editions; structural coverage is not mathematical correctness, runtime conformance, or an empirical utility score.

**Evidence:** Current source-section review and targeted comparison with the 589-page checked-in PDF; exact snapshots and 29 detailed findings retained in this record audit data.; Decision-odds counterexample independently recomputed using exact rational arithmetic. No claim of rerunning every cited proof or historical experiment.

**Next action:** Finish Paper1 visual gate and hosted publication validation; twelve scoped corrections resolved, remaining research imports stay open.

**Completion condition:** Every incorporated result names its observation model, assumptions, decision consequence and source witness.; Correct formulas, comparator roles and output complexity agree across paper, chapter, figure and index.; Every intended Book PDF is generated from the accepted sources and checked for the corrected statements.; Uncertain-answer acquisition, field detection, replay fidelity and reviewer efficacy remain open until their declared experiments or refinements pass.

**Dependencies:** NEXT-P1; NEXT-P5; NEXT-P6; NEXT-P7; NEXT-P8; RELEASE-FACTORIZATION; BOOK-PROOFS

**Completion condition:** All seven named audit disagreements have an explicit corrected statement and check or a clearly delimited unresolved proof obligation.; Source/PDF and index discrepancies have current file-bound receipts.; New admission result states reachability provenance and does not treat incompatible futures as jointly reachable.; Every proposed paper has a central question, strongest result, nearest baseline, utility test and a merge/split condition.

**Dependencies:** BOOK-RESEARCH-UPTAKE

**Correction dispositions:**

- **Decision odds:** Correct attention-adjusted denominator; handle degenerate costs without invalid division.
- **Strict zoom savings:** Strict d<1/12, equality break-even for displayed bound.
- **Split-floor proof:** Increasing log-ratio summands; varying-parameter growth, no model/store necessity.
- **Escalation threshold:** Continuity, strictness, crossing and tie policy; possibly empty feasible interval.
- **Paging claim:** Imported exact Landlord theorem separated from corrupted-pin conjecture.
- **Conflict complexity and authority:** Scope incidences and output cost; NP-hardness does not mandate a human judge.
- **Recovery equivalence:** Preservation of score/obligations separated from behavior and remote-effect safety.
- **Counting-bound achievability:** Upper bound only; explicit N4/k2/m3/B1 unattainability witness.
- **Ranking ties:** Strict total-order theorem, compatible weak ties explained.
- **Lower bound versus achieved cost:** Single-reader and joint-union floors do not establish an encoder or a locate-write capability.
- **Scalar embedding scope:** Finite candidate set and shared deterministic tie rule explicitly required.
- **Miscalibration direction:** With g(s)=s², a naive posterior threshold acts too early; calibration raises the score threshold. With g(s)=sqrt(s), the naive rule acts too late. Direct expected-loss witnesses verify both directions.

**Next bounded research protocol:**

- **Name:** Two-operation continuation with delayed outcomes
- **Scope:** A finite model and adapter contract test; no live agent execution or spend.
- **State:** Two stable operation IDs, two authority epochs, a durable request/result ledger, in-flight/committed/acknowledged state per operation, and a replayed local checkpoint.
- **Faults:** ["crash before dispatch", "crash after commit before receipt", "late prior-epoch commit after negative readback", "duplicate reply", "reordered replies for distinct operations", "operation-ID aliasing"]
- **Baselines:** ["unconditional retry after timeout", "current-epoch check without remote fencing", "atomic adapter deduplication and stored result", "fenced authoritative readback"]
- **Safety:** ["at most one external effect per logical operation", "receipt binds operation ID, payload digest and epoch", "no stale actor effect after completed fence"]
- **Progress:** State the fairness and adapter-availability assumptions; report unresolved outcomes separately from violations.
- **Oracle:** Enumerate finite transition traces and compare admitted actions with the set safe in every observationally equivalent remote state.
- **Mutants:** ["remove atomic dedup/result coupling", "drop payload binding", "allow a late commit after the fence", "treat negative unfenced readback as final absence"]
- **Stop rule:** A counterexample narrows or rejects the contract; a finite pass does not certify an unbounded distributed implementation.

**Execution gates:**

- scientific-corrections: twelve scoped corrections complete; nine exact boundary tests pass
- publication-parity: local PDF/source parity and visual checks pass; hosted pinned renderer pending
- reachable-antichain-result: proof and finite oracle complete; reachability provider and field validation open
- publication-portfolio: six candidate decision problems defined; physical manuscript regrouping gated

**Candidate publication portfolio** (source numbers are locators, not a fixed paper count):

#### Oversight under an inspection budget

**Source material:** ["1"]

**Decision question:** Which evidence must a human open to meet a declared miss/loss budget?

**Strongest result:** Covering floor, scalar-order incompatibility and explicit zoom bound under group-oracle assumptions.

**Baseline:** Shared-vector separate-head summaries; flat/direct inspection; established group testing.

**Utility test:** Held-out incidents, equal evidence, calibration and total operator time; count false silence.

**Packaging gate:** Keep one paper if all claims serve the same oversight decision; move paging conjecture to an appendix until proved.

#### Safe continuation across lost outcomes

**Source material:** ["2", "5"]

**Decision question:** When may a successor safely continue an external effect?

**Strongest result:** Observation ambiguity plus adapter-specific deduplication/fencing conditions; mediated prevention boundary.

**Baseline:** Durable outbox, atomic idempotency ledger and fenced readback under the identical fault model.

**Utility test:** Two operations, crashes and late effects; prove no duplicate/stale effect and report unresolved liveness separately.

**Packaging gate:** Combine control and recovery material around one adapter contract; leave economic identity in the incentives paper.

#### Verification, identity and delegated labor

**Source material:** ["3", "5", "6"]

**Decision question:** When do inspection and liability make delegation incentive-compatible?

**Strongest result:** Scoped no-mint, engine-substitution and inspection threshold results; specialization only under its queueing model.

**Baseline:** Fixed audit rates, direct verification and established principal-agent models.

**Utility test:** Calibrated detection/penalty/cost estimates with sensitivity and adversarial selection tests.

**Packaging gate:** Split queueing economics only if it independently changes a staffing decision and earns empirical support.

#### Controlled release from a sealed workspace

**Source material:** ["4"]

**Decision question:** What information may a workspace release while honoring its privacy contract?

**Strongest result:** Noninterference and atomic budget accounting under an explicit release map.

**Baseline:** Standard DP composition and information-flow release mechanisms.

**Utility test:** Payload factorization and concurrent release failures; verify both content and budget, not alphabet size alone.

**Packaging gate:** Keep privacy separate because its security assumptions and evaluation differ from coordination incentives.

#### Admission under changing facts

**Source material:** ["6"]

**Decision question:** When can a commitment be accepted safely across declared future states?

**Strongest result:** Tractable fragment with output accounting and maximal-reachable-family safety test.

**Baseline:** Batch Horn closure and SAT/SMT with explicit timeout/abstention.

**Utility test:** Reachability provenance, incompatible futures, incremental workload and output-sensitive performance.

**Packaging gate:** Extract as a focused systems/logic paper if reachability-provider integration works; otherwise a rigorous admission chapter.

#### Evidence sufficient for diagnosis

**Source material:** ["7", "8"]

**Decision question:** Which retained or acquired observations justify contradiction, attribution or safe refusal?

**Strongest result:** Exact categorical certificates, least-cost retained witnesses and typed report-error distance; Hodge decomposition is a diagnostic representation.

**Baseline:** Equal-information raw claim checks, categorical feasibility, graph coding distance and established sheaf theory.

**Utility test:** Authenticated telemetry, held-out fault classes, topology versus direct-claim/anchor acquisition at equal cost.

**Packaging gate:** Develop Papers7/8 as one candidate with typed/relative topology extensions only where they change a decision; split only if the topology result has independent utility beyond the categorical baseline.

**Sources:** [whitepaper/textbook.json](../../whitepaper/textbook.json) — chapter authority; [docs/harbor-research/library-index.json](library-index.json) — result identity and paper/chapter homes

<a id="work-book-crdt"></a>
### BOOK-CRDT: Steel-man rail versus CRDT/merge queue

**Status:** open. **Kind:** comparative-research-plan.

**Result or question:** Commitment correctness and effect mediation are distinct from convergent editing; evidence plane can remain convergent. S2 is the named falsifier.

### CLAIM-EXACT: Exact categorical feasibility before linear residual

**Status:** scoped manuscript result or correction checked; local publication verified. **Kind:** proposed checker.

**Result or question:** For one finite categorical fact and snapshot, a valid zero/one-hot-difference edge table has a categorical potential iff every zero-edge component has at most one distinct pin. Missing reports are omitted; the linear residual can vanish on an impossible categorical table.

**Assumptions:** One fact, finite domain, reciprocal same-snapshot messages, signed one-hot differences.

**Evidence:** Research note gives path eB-eA repeated on two edges as witness.; 2026-09-30 source reports 17 named audit tests including exhaustive K3 tables and new dependent-triangle regression; path3 defeats zero real residual.

**Next action:** Admit authenticated schema-bound receipts and compare against raw sender-claim checking on identical inputs; zero differences can erase values.

**Completion condition:** Every finding has two pins plus zero path verified against admitted rows.

<a id="work-future-horn"></a>
### FUTURE-HORN: Future facts can activate accepted conflicts

**Status:** scoped manuscript result or correction checked; local publication verified. **Kind:** proposed theorem.

**Result or question:** For fixed positive Horn rules and monotone conflict on a finite nonempty explicitly supplied reachable family containing current facts, universal future safety is equivalent to safety of every inclusion-maximal reachable set. Mutually exclusive futures remain separate.

**Assumptions:** Rules/intervals/resources fixed, only positive facts grow, maximum jointly reachable.

**Evidence:** Research note records three-transition witness and bounded enumeration.; 2026-09-30 note proves lemma and reports 2,048 Horn-oracle and 8,192 envelope checks.

**Next action:** Supply reachability independently; test a two-operation adapter state model and incremental antichain maintenance without assuming reachability is polynomial.

**Completion condition:** Reject union-envelope exactness when future facts are mutually exclusive.

**Dependencies:** R17-B4; R5

**Sources:** [docs/harbor-research/pdf/paper6.pdf](#source-src-128) — pp. 5-6; [docs/harbor-research/pdf/paper2.pdf](#source-src-124) — pp. 8-9; [docs/harbor-research/research/research-program-20260930.md](#source-src-142) — Maximal-fact admission theorem — proved here

**Status:** scoped manuscript result or correction checked; local publication verified. **Kind:** algebraic-correction.

**Evidence:** 17,545 exact triples checked; old numerator-superadditivity explanation refuted.

### SUBSTRATE-CORPUS: Corpus bridging, changed windows and pilot reproducibility

**Status:** completed-source-reported. **Kind:** method-correction.

This changes the original per-commit workload interpretation and makes drop counts and exact task construction essential to reproduce.

**Assumptions:** Source-reported corpora are nestjs/nest, psf/requests and rust-lang/book; bridge diffs can aggregate dropped-commit changes.

**Evidence:** CHANGELOG documents first-parent pinning, 950/6500/850 raw windows, revised kept counts and rerun of pilot; README still says pilot seeds 1-3 whereas PILOT/CHANGELOG say 1-2.

**Next action:** Correct README seed count; version corpus/task hashes and report bridging effect on overlap, size and task semantics before H2 analysis.

**Completion condition:** Same pinned task lists and CSV cells regenerate, README matches actual grid, and altered workload is explained in REPORT.

<a id="work-substrate-d-defect"></a>
### SUBSTRATE-D-DEFECT: Merge-queue simulation silently drops out-of-order patches

**Status:** open-blocker. **Kind:** implementation-defect.

**Result or question:** D prepares commits from historical parent while bridging patches assume prior kept tasks; out-of-order completion permits rebase to drop a task as no-op, then queue head stalls and later tasks fail, yielding invariant four landings. Conflict-marker-only tearing and evidence=1 miss lost content.

**Assumptions:** Defect isolated by source-reported direct d_try_land reproduction; smoke does not exercise adjacent overlapping commits.

**Evidence:** CHANGELOG documents task 0,2,1,4 reproduction and CPU-bound N8 cells; PILOT rejects D comparisons.

**Next action:** Define queue semantics for predecessor dependence, assert each accepted task's expected content/effect survives, add adversarial overlapping out-of-order fixture, then rerun all D cells.

**Completion condition:** A same-seed shuffled completion order cannot silently accept a no-op as landed; D completes bounded cells and content-preservation oracle passes.

**Dependencies:** SUBSTRATE-S2

**Sources:** [studies/substrate-study/CHANGELOG.md](#source-src-164) — D still lands exactly 4/200; [studies/substrate-study/PILOT.md](#source-src-165) — H1; H2

**Status:** open-protocol. **Kind:** research-protocol.

**Evidence:** The dive enumerated all working-paper propositions and reproduced theorem arithmetic.

**Assumptions:** Ledger serves adaptive heterogeneous release requests.

**Evidence:** Primary Rogers et al. text read; Theorem 3.4 distinguishes valid basic filter from invalid advanced shortcut.

**Next action:** Replace corollary with a valid adaptive filter theorem or narrow it to fixed schedules.

**Completion condition:** A proof covers the actual adaptive budget model and an adversarial parameter-choice test fails under the old rule.

<a id="work-review-synthesis-05"></a>
### review-synthesis-05: Paper 5 adverse selection is not disclosure unraveling

**Status:** historically_adjudicated. **Kind:** conceptual_correction.

**Result or question:** Theorem 2a is an Akerlof pooling/adverse-selection result, whereas Grossman-Milgrom unraveling concludes full disclosure. Grossman spans the two endpoints using disclosure cost, which Paper 5 does not model. Mandatory attestation needs a named failed voluntary-disclosure hypothesis.

**Assumptions:** Agents may be unable to attest, creating cannot-versus-will-not ambiguity.

**Evidence:** Milgrom Proposition 6 and Grossman sections 2-3 read directly.

**Next action:** Rename theorem and model/qualify the disclosure-cost crossover.

**Completion condition:** Novel claim states which unraveling assumption fails and why protocol-mandated attestation changes incentives.

**Evidence:** Full working paper was read in 2026-08-27 follow-up; original adverse hypothesis retracted.
